# 2026-09-29 — Branch Protection, GPU Retention, ADR-0011 Work Governance, Jira Executive Sync, and External Agent Base Execution Plan

**Primary agent:** STEA-004 Entrepreneur  
**Date:** 2026-09-29  
**Purpose:** Execute the human decisions made after `FINAL-HANDOFF-20260929.md`, then continue the highest-value already-approved ACMS / Server Manager / LLM Manager work without reopening resolved architecture questions.

---

# 1. Human decisions now resolved

These decisions are authoritative for this execution window.

## 1.1 Credential rotation — DEFER

**Decision:** Do **not** rotate credentials now.

Reason:

```text
no active security threat has been identified
rotation is useful future maintenance
it is not urgent enough to interrupt current engineering work
```

Required action:

- leave the existing credential-rotation runbook intact;
- keep credential rotation in Future Work / technical debt / Human Attention;
- do not rotate or revoke current Bridge/LLM worker credentials in this sprint;
- do not repeatedly raise it as an urgent blocker unless new evidence of compromise appears.

If an actual compromise or active threat is discovered, stop and escalate.

---

## 1.2 Branch protection — APPROVED

**Decision:** Protect every repository/branch that AI agents actively modify.

Required policy for protected `main`/production branches:

```text
pull request required
required CI/status checks must pass
normal AI-agent identities may NOT bypass protection
normal AI-agent work must NOT push directly to main
automated AI PR creation/review/merge is allowed
human approval is NOT required on every PR
human administrators retain emergency/direct-main authority
force-push disabled for normal agent identities
branch deletion disabled for protected production branches
```

Important intent:

> Branch protection exists to prevent accidental direct pushes, not to force a human to click “Approve” for every autonomous change.

AI agents may continue the successful current workflow:

```text
branch
→ implementation
→ tests
→ PR
→ required checks
→ automated/self review
→ merge when green
→ safe deployment
```

Human administrators may bypass in emergencies or intentionally commit directly when necessary.

---

## 1.3 GPU telemetry retention — APPROVED

Use the previously proposed **Option A**.

### Raw data

```text
gpu_samples raw retention: 90 days
```

### Aggregated data

```text
hourly summaries retention: 2 years
```

### Partitioning

PostgreSQL partitioning is **deferred** until database size, ingest rate, or query performance demonstrates a real need.

Record partitioning as technical debt / future scaling work.

### Pruning authorization

Automatic pruning of raw `gpu_samples` older than 90 days is authorized **only after**:

```text
hourly rollup table exists
backfill completes
aggregation is verified against raw samples
backup/recovery point exists
retention is configuration-backed
dry-run pruning result is reviewed programmatically
rollback procedure exists
```

Never prune first and aggregate later.

---

## 1.4 ADR-0011 — ACCEPTED

**ADR-0011 is accepted.**

Canonical work semantics:

```text
one ACMS Work Item
=
one measurable goal with a measurable deliverable
```

A Work Item may contain:

```text
multiple execution sessions
multiple /new context rotations
multiple tasks
multiple worker agents
multiple commits
multiple PRs when necessary
```

provided they all contribute to the same measurable goal.

A `/new` caused by context size, model/session rotation, or interruption does **not** create a new Work Item if the measurable goal remains unchanged.

When the measurable goal changes:

```text
create a new Work Item
```

Work Items may share a higher-level:

```text
Jira issue
Project
Feature
Program
human business goal
```

### Work creation authority

**Executive Agent:**

```text
MAY create additional ACMS Work Items
MAY decompose an approved higher-level goal
MAY assign/delegate those Work Items
MAY create internal tasks/assignments
MUST stay within approved scope/budget
```

**Subordinate worker agents:**

```text
MAY execute assigned work
MAY create implementation subtasks/checklists inside their Work Item
MAY produce handoffs/recommend next work
MAY NOT autonomously create new ACMS Work Items
MAY NOT silently expand scope
```

If a worker identifies new work:

```text
worker records recommendation in handoff
→ Executive Agent decides whether to create a new Work Item
```

This is intended to prevent recursive/unbounded work creation.

---

## 1.5 Jira / ACMS integration — APPROVED DIRECTION

Jira remains the **human-facing planning system**.

ACMS is the **AI execution/orchestration system beneath it**.

Canonical relationship:

```text
Human
→ Jira issue / Epic / Task
→ assigned conceptually to Executive Agent
→ ACMS creates internal Work Items
→ Executive Agent delegates to workers
→ ACMS tracks detailed execution
→ summaries / links / useful status flow back to Jira
```

### Jira ticket policy

Do **not** create a Jira ticket for every AI microtask.

Do **not** create “bazillions” of Jira issues corresponding to internal Work Items.

Instead:

```text
1 Jira item
→ potentially many ACMS Work Items/tasks
```

ACMS internal decomposition stays internal.

### Jira updates allowed

The integration may automatically:

```text
read Jira work assigned to the designated AI/Executive account
read Jira issue descriptions/comments/context
attach ACMS/Jira correlation IDs
add useful status comments
add handoff/result summaries
add links to artifacts/PRs
report completion/blockers
```

For v1, avoid complex bidirectional workflow automation unless necessary.

Do not automatically create child Jira tickets for ACMS work.

Do not build Linear as the primary integration.

### Jira vs Linear

Current direction:

```text
Jira = primary human work-management system
Linear = existing synchronized secondary system / fallback
```

Reason:

- Jira supports software and non-software work;
- Jira is highly customizable;
- Linear is primarily software-oriented;
- required Linear features would add unnecessary cost;
- Startup Teams already has Jira↔Linear synchronization if Linear becomes useful later.

Do not spend engineering time replacing Jira with Linear.

### Jira account

A dedicated full Jira account already exists for:

```text
miamtechnologypvtltd@gmail.com
```

The Executive Agent integration may use the authorized Jira account/service identity to:

```text
see work assigned by humans
read issue context
post comments/status summaries
link ACMS execution results
```

Never place Jira passwords, API tokens, recovery codes, or session cookies in Git.

Prefer API token/OAuth/service authentication over interactive browser login where supported.

---

## 1.6 `agentifyme-external-agent-base` — CREATION APPROVED

Create a **private** repository:

```text
startupteams/agentifyme-external-agent-base
```

Purpose:

> Temporary safe baseline for external/customer-facing AgentifyMe workers, including Speed-to-Lead workers.

This is explicitly a **temporary architecture**, not necessarily the final long-term Agent State solution.

### Allowed reuse from `hermes-base-setup`

Sanitized generic mechanisms may be copied/adapted:

```text
.gitignore strategy
secret scanning
safe bootstrap mechanics
version/build metadata
state-sync patterns
generic deployment helpers
generic documentation structure
```

### Explicitly forbidden content

Do **not** copy:

```text
Startup Teams internal profiles
SOUL/persona files containing internal company context
internal memories
internal skills
company-only instructions
internal customer information
API keys/tokens/secrets
employee-specific context
private operational notes
```

### One-time contamination validation

During repository creation:

1. create the sanitized baseline in a temporary worktree/directory;
2. scan it for known internal profile/memory/skill markers;
3. inspect Git history/content before first production use;
4. fail the setup if internal Startup Teams content is found;
5. correct the source tree before use.

A permanent continuous “internal marker” test is **not required** at this time.

Normal secret scanning remains required.

### Speed-to-Lead

Speed-to-Lead worker agents may temporarily use this repository as their default agent baseline.

Do not copy the internal `hermes-base-setup` agent branches into it.

# 3. Phase A — enforce AI-safe branch protection

This is now explicitly authorized.

## A1. Inventory repositories AI agents modify

At minimum inspect:

```text
startupteams/acms-project-framework
startupteams/llm-manager-project-framework
startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework
startupteams/hermes-base-setup
startupteams/agentifyme-speed-to-lead
```

Also include any other repository currently modified by autonomous AI agents.

Do not blindly modify unrelated archival repositories.

## A2. Protection policy

For each active production/default branch:

```text
require pull request before merge
require existing CI/status checks
AI/machine identity cannot push directly to protected branch
AI/machine identity cannot bypass required checks
no normal force push
no normal branch deletion
```

Do **not** require a human approving review merely to merge routine AI-generated PRs unless that repository already has such a requirement.

Preserve:

```text
human administrator emergency bypass/direct-main capability
```

Document who/what is considered:

```text
human admin
AI agent identity
deployment identity
CI identity
```

## A3. Verification

Perform a harmless verification:

```text
AI identity direct push → rejected
PR with failing required check → cannot merge
PR with all required checks → automated merge path works
human admin emergency path remains documented/available
```

Do not intentionally break production to test this.

## A4. Documentation

Create/update:

```text
docs/operations/BRANCH_PROTECTION_AND_AI_MERGE_POLICY.md
```

Checkpoint.

# 4. Phase B — execute approved GPU retention architecture

Implement Option A safely.

## B1. Re-measure before migration

Record:

```text
row count
table size
database size
oldest/newest sample
approx rows/day
approx MB/day
important query patterns
```

Do not assume the last handoff's values are still exact.

## B2. Create hourly rollup

Add an authoritative aggregate table, such as:

```text
gpu_samples_hourly
```

Use the actual existing schema and metrics rather than inventing columns.

Aggregation must preserve the operational statistics required by LLM Manager.

Typical concepts may include where supported:

```text
host/GPU identity
hour bucket
sample_count
min/max/avg utilization
memory metrics
temperature
power
other currently queried telemetry
```

## B3. Backfill

Backfill all existing raw history.

Use a resumable/idempotent strategy if practical.

## B4. Verification

For representative windows compare raw calculations against rollup rows.

Require deterministic tolerance rules for numeric aggregation.

No pruning until verification passes.

## B5. Retention configuration

Configure:

```text
raw retention = 90 days
hourly rollup retention = 730 days / 2 years
```

Values should be configuration-backed rather than embedded in a one-off SQL script.

## B6. Backup

Confirm recoverable backup/recovery point before first destructive prune.

## B7. Dry-run prune

Calculate exactly:

```text
cutoff
candidate row count
candidate size
oldest row after prune
```

Log the dry-run result.

## B8. Execute pruning

After all gates above pass:

```text
delete only raw rows older than configured retention
retain hourly aggregates
```

Use bounded transactions/batches if table behavior requires it.

## B9. Scheduled maintenance

Add a scheduled rollup + retention job with:

```text
locking
idempotency
metrics/logs
failure alert/Attention path
```

## B10. Partitioning

Do not partition now.

Create/retain TDR/Future Work trigger such as:

```text
partition if:
table growth materially accelerates
retention delete becomes operationally expensive
query latency crosses threshold
maintenance/vacuum becomes problematic
```

Checkpoint.

---

# 5. Phase C — accept and enforce ADR-0011 governance

## C1. ADR

Change ADR-0011:

```text
Proposed → Accepted
```

Include the human clarification from this plan.

## C2. Canonical hierarchy

Document:

```text
human business goal / Jira issue / Feature
    ↓
Executive Agent
    ├─ Work Item A = measurable goal
    │   ├─ task/session/worker
    │   ├─ task/session/worker
    │   └─ measurable deliverable
    ├─ Work Item B = measurable goal
    └─ Work Item C = measurable goal
```

A Work Item may use multiple agents.

## C3. Creation authorization

Enforce at API/service layer if the current roles support it cleanly:

```text
Administrator → may create Work Items
Executive Agent → may create Work Items within delegated scope
ordinary Worker → may NOT create Work Items
Observer → may NOT create Work Items
```

Do not rely only on UI hiding.

If “Executive Agent” is not currently a first-class authorization concept, implement the smallest explicit capability/delegation model rather than checking an agent's display name.

## C4. Worker recommendation path

Workers need a safe way to say:

```text
I found additional work
```

without creating it.

Reuse handoff/Attention/proposal mechanisms where practical.

Recommended structure:

```text
proposed_next_work:
  summary
  reason
  dependency
  suggested acceptance criteria
```

Executive Agent decides whether to turn it into a Work Item.

## C5. `/new` behavior

Do not create new Work merely because:

```text
context rotated
session restarted
model changed
agent process restarted
```

if the measurable goal remains the same.

Checkpoint.

---

# 6. Phase D — Jira ↔ Executive Agent v1 integration

Implement the approved **Jira-human / ACMS-execution** model.

## D1. Architecture

```text
Jira issue
  = human-facing work / goal

Executive Agent
  = conceptual AI assignee/orchestrator

ACMS Work Items
  = internal AI decomposition

Worker tasks/sessions
  = internal execution
```

## D2. Authentication

Use the designated authorized Jira account/service identity.

Never embed credentials in source.

Support environment/secret-managed configuration.

If a Jira API token/site URL/project mapping is not available to the agent:

- implement/test the integration contract with mock/sandbox configuration;
- create an exact human setup note;
- do not fabricate credentials.

## D3. Inbound synchronization

Implement a bounded poll/webhook integration that can detect Jira work assigned to the Executive Agent/service identity.

Capture:

```text
Jira issue key
summary
description
priority
labels
status
human comments/context
links/attachments metadata where safely supported
```

Create/link the corresponding high-level ACMS work context.

Do not create hundreds of ACMS Work Items automatically from arbitrary Jira text without Executive decomposition.

## D4. Executive decomposition

The Executive Agent may:

```text
read assigned Jira issue
understand human goal
create one or more ACMS Work Items
delegate them internally
track progress
```

Subordinate workers do not create Jira issues.

## D5. Outbound Jira updates

Automatically post useful summaries rather than noisy event streams.

Examples:

```text
execution started
major milestone completed
blocked / Human Attention required
PR/artifact available
goal completed
final handoff summary
```

Include links/correlation IDs where possible.

Do not post every tool call/session event.

## D6. Jira status mutation

For v1, keep Jira status changes conservative.

Comments/summaries/links are authorized.

If automatic Jira status transitions are necessary, implement behind an explicit config flag defaulting OFF unless an existing human workflow clearly defines the transitions.

## D7. No Jira microtask explosion

Explicit invariant:

```text
ACMS Work Item creation
≠
Jira ticket creation
```

Do not create separate Jira tickets for normal internal AI decomposition.

## D8. Linear

Do not implement a new Linear-native path now.

Document that existing Jira↔Linear synchronization remains sufficient fallback.

Checkpoint.

---

## E1. Create repo

startupteams/agentifyme-external-agent-base
```

## E2. Build from sanitized generic mechanisms

Use `hermes-base-setup` only as a **source of generic patterns**.

USER.md internal employee content
MEMORY.md internal context
profiles/
memories/
skills/
Startup Teams internal operating instructions
customer/internal names not intended for product baseline
secrets

tokens

```

Do not rely only on filenames; inspect contents.

## E4. One-time contamination audit

Produce:

```text
SANITIZATION_AUDIT_2026-09-29.md
```

containing:

```text

files intentionally excluded
secret scan result
internal-marker scan result
manual inspection result
Git history check
```

If contamination is discovered before first use:

```text
remove it
rewrite/recreate repository history as needed
verify clean again
```

A permanent custom “Startup Teams marker” test is not required.

Normal secret scanning remains continuous.

## E5. External worker profile

Baseline should be intentionally blank/minimal:

```text
no Startup Teams persona
no internal memories
no internal skills
no company-specific instructions
```

Product-specific configuration is layered later by Speed-to-Lead or another external product.

## E6. Documentation

State clearly:

> This repository is a temporary safe baseline for external AgentifyMe workers. It is not assumed to be the final long-term Agent State architecture.

## E7. Speed-to-Lead

Update architectural documentation so new Speed-to-Lead worker provisioning targets this baseline rather than internal `hermes-base-setup`.

Do not migrate production customer workers if none exist yet.

Checkpoint.

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous coding agents working in this repository.

## 1. Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, and approve merges.

Agents may:

- inspect the repository;
- propose a short plan;
- implement approved requirements;
- create or update automated tests;
- update documentation affected by the change;
- add newly discovered ideas to `FUTURE_WORK.md`;
- draft ADRs and TDRs;
- run non-destructive validation;
- prepare commits and pull requests.

Agents must not:

- silently add product scope;
- promote future work into approved requirements without human approval;
- accept their own newly invented significant architecture decision;
- merge their own work under the current operating model;
- commit directly to `main`/production branches;
- use destructive commands, rotate secrets, alter production data, change permissions, or modify infrastructure without explicit human approval;
- hide failed validation, security concerns, or unresolved trade-offs.

## 2. Read before planning

For every meaningful change, read the relevant parts of:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
5. relevant files in `docs/adr/`
6. relevant files in `docs/tdr/`
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope or follow-up work is involved

Repository-local instructions and approved requirements are the primary source of truth.

## 3. Requirement discipline

Implementation work should trace to one or more `REQ-###` entries.

Before editing, summarize:

- the requirement IDs being implemented;
- the acceptance criteria;
- the files/components likely to change;
- assumptions or ambiguities;
- the validation that will prove the change works.

If the requested work is not covered by an approved requirement, do one of the following:

- ask for human approval to add/modify the requirement; or
- record the idea in `FUTURE_WORK.md` if it is not part of the current scope.

Do not invent scope merely because it seems useful.

## 4. Planning and execution

Before meaningful edits, provide a short implementation plan. Keep the plan proportional to the change.

During execution:

- work in a dedicated branch/worktree/sandbox;
- keep the diff focused;
- follow the existing architecture and project conventions;
- prefer readable, conventional code over clever code;
- avoid unrelated cleanup;
- preserve backward compatibility unless an approved requirement or ADR says otherwise;
- update affected documentation in the same change.

If the implementation grows materially beyond the approved plan, stop and surface the scope increase.

## 5. Significant decisions and ADRs

Create or update an ADR when a change makes a significant, durable decision about architecture, major dependencies, data/storage, external integrations, security boundaries, deployment model, scalability approach, or another choice that future maintainers are likely to ask "why did we choose this?"

### Human-directed decision

If a human explicitly made the decision, the agent may record it as `Status: Accepted`. The ADR must still be called out in the pull request so the human sees what was recorded.

### Agent-originated decision

If the agent concludes a significant decision is needed, create a draft ADR with `Status: Proposed` and surface it for human approval. Do not treat it as approved until a human accepts it.

Use `docs/adr/TEMPLATE.md`.

## 6. Technical debt and TDRs

Create a TDR when a change intentionally leaves known technical debt such as a shortcut, workaround, temporary design, missing test, manual step, or deferred reliability/security/performance/maintainability improvement.

Use `docs/tdr/TEMPLATE.md` and keep the record concise.

An agent may propose a TDR, but the human reviewer should confirm that the debt is acceptable and that the owner is appropriate.

## 7. Validation

Use the exact validation commands documented by the project once the implementation stack is selected.

Run all checks relevant to the change, such as:

- formatting;
- lint/static analysis;
- type checking where applicable;
- unit tests;
- integration tests;
- end-to-end tests where applicable;
- build/package validation;
- security/secret/dependency checks;
- deployment or readiness checks when relevant.

Report exactly what passed, failed, or was not run. Never imply that a check passed if it was skipped or unavailable.

If expected commands are not documented, surface that as an agent-readiness gap instead of guessing silently.

## 8. Security and data handling

- Do not commit secrets, credentials, private keys, access tokens, or sensitive customer/company data.
- Treat generated shell commands, migrations, dependencies, and configuration changes as untrusted until reviewed.
- Do not weaken authentication, authorization, encryption, logging, isolation, or monitoring merely to make a task easier.
- Surface security-sensitive behavior for explicit human review.

## 9. Git and pull requests

Startup Teams Git policy applies unless a stricter project policy is documented.

- Use a dedicated branch; direct commits to `main`/production branches are prohibited.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description` (for example, `feat/AGR-24-bnpl-checkout`).
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Commit scope is required.
- Every commit must reference the related issue/ticket.
- Keep commits and PRs small enough to review.
- Required validation must pass before merge.
- A human reviewer must approve before merge under the current operating model.

Every PR should state:

- feature / requirement IDs;
- what changed and why;
- files/components changed;
- validation performed and results;
- known risks or limitations;
- documentation changes;
- ADRs/TDRs added or changed;
- future-work items discovered;
- reviewer focus areas.

## 10. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict or are materially ambiguous;
- a new significant ADR decision is required;
- credentials or restricted access are required;
- a destructive action is needed;
- tests fail unexpectedly and the cause is not understood;
- security-sensitive behavior changes;
- production data or infrastructure would be changed;
- the diff grows substantially beyond the planned scope;
- a future-work idea would need to become approved product scope.

## 11. Handoff standard

At the end of a work session or before handing work to another agent/human, provide:

1. requirement IDs addressed;
2. concise summary of changes;
3. files changed;
4. validation commands and results;
5. known risks / unresolved questions;
6. ADR/TDR status;
7. future-work items discovered;
8. recommended next action.

## 12. PDU Manager production safety additions

This application can control real electrical power. Treat production interactions as high-consequence infrastructure operations.

Agents MUST NOT perform real PDU ON/OFF/REBOOT/CYCLE operations as tests unless a human explicitly authorizes the exact outlet/action for that session.

During normal development, source capture, CI, staging, or deployment verification:

- use mocks/fixtures for actuation behavior;
- prefer read-only outlet-state checks when technically enforceable;
- do not reboot VM154 or `MIAM-00133` merely to apply application changes;
- restart only the application service(s) when a service restart is sufficient;
- do not modify production outlet labels, presets, protection/lockout settings, or credentials as an incidental test;
- never place PDU/LDAP/TLS credentials in Git;
- preserve protection of critical power targets;
- treat any change that could remove a safety interlock as security/safety sensitive and stop for human review.

The production endpoint is `https://10.0.20.156/` on VM156 hosted by `MIAM-00133` (post-cutover 2026-09-27; VM154 is powered-off fallback). This location is an approved deployment target, not permission for unrestricted infrastructure changes.

## 13. Har

[Subdirectory context discovered: work/acms-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preser

{"output": "branch 'feat/adr0011-accepted-work-creation-guards' set up to track 'origin/main'.\n# ADR-0011 — Measurable-goal Work lifecycle\n\n- **Status:** Proposed (agent-originated; human acceptance pending)\n- **Date:** 2026-09-29\n- **Deciders:** STEA-004 (author), Jordan Ulmer (acceptance pending)\n- **Related:** ACMS-REQ-055, REQ-013, REQ-057, REQ-059; `docs/architecture/MEASURABLE_GOAL_WORK_LIFECYCLE.md`\n\n## Context\n\nHuman direction (2026-09-29): work should be organized as *measurable goal → execute → handoff →\nnext measurable goal as a new plan/fresh session*. ACMS already supports session continuity inside\na Work Item (REQ-055) and a parent/child work hierarchy, but the default decomposition policy was\nnever codified.\n\n## Decision (Proposed)\n\n1. **One Work Item = one measurable goal** with explicit acceptance criteria and its own budget.\n2. **Sequential goals = child Work Items** under the same parent Feature/Program (`parent_id`;\n   no schema change). Lineage convention: reference the predecessor Work key in title/scope.\n3. **Execution sessions remain disposable inside a Work Item** (REQ-055/056/057): rotation,\n   crash-retry, context-rotation never end the Work.\n4. **Executive Agent next-work creation** (where authorized): completed Work + complete handoff →\n   next child Work Item under the same parent; provenance recorded; out-of-scope findings become\n   proposals, never silent scope changes.\n5. **Review findings classify BLOCKING / REQUIRED / OPTIONAL; OPTIONAL never blocks completion;\n   review never creates new product scope.** No hard review-loop count.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- No code/schema change required (documentation + convention).\n- Budgets stay per-goal (REQ-059) — finer cost attribution per accepted output.\n- Conserves REQ-055 semantics; does not prejudge the open Work↔Session boundary (workbook A/B/C).\n- If accepted, SPRINT/REQUIREMENTS get a pointer; if rejected, the doc is withdrawn with no code impact.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n- **Multi-goal Work Items** (rejected: blurs budget attribution and \"definition of done\").\n- **New lineage schema (successor_work_item_id)** (rejected: over-engineering; event trail +\n  naming convention reconstructs lineage without migration risk).", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

### [2026-09-29T09:58:51.015111+00:00] assistant

Now implementing Phase C: ADR-0011 → Accepted plus Executive-only Work Item creation enforcement. The design:

1. **ADR doc**: Proposed → Accepted with the human clarifications (work-creation authority matrix, `/new` behavior, worker recommendation path).
2. **Code guard**: `POST /api/v1/work/items` gains a `created_by_authority` check — a `created_by` value starting with `agent:` (subordinate worker) is rejected 403 unless it's a registered Executive identity; Administrators (bearer token) can always create. Workers get the documented `proposed_next_work` handoff path instead. Audited via a `WORK_CREATION_REJECTED` event.
3. Worker recommendation structure documented in the handoff model.

Checking the handoff model for where to add `proposed_next_work`:

{"status": "success", "output": "async def api_create_work_item(\n    payload: WorkItemCreate,\n    db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session),\n    _: None = Depends(require_admin_token),\n) -> WorkItemResponse:\n    # ADR-0011 (Accepted): Work Item creation is Administrator/Executive-only.\n    # Workers record proposed_next_work in handoffs; they never create Work Items.\n    decision = evaluate_work_item_creation(caller_is_admin=True, created_by=payload.created_by)\n    if not decision.allowed:\n        await TelemetryService.record_event(\n            db,\n            event_type=\"WORK_CREATION_REJECTED\",\n            summary=\"Work Item creation rejected: \" + decision.reason,\n            actor_source=\"work_api\",\n            work_key=None,\n            metadata={\"created_by\": payload.created_by, \"authority\": decision.authority},\n        )\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail=decision.reason)\n\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 0.02}

{"status": "success", "output": "\nclass TelemetryService:\n    \"\"\"Stateless-ish helpers over the telemetry tables; session injected.\"\"\"\n\n    @staticmethod\n    async def record_event(\n        db: AsyncSession, *, event_type: str, summary: str, agent_id: str | None = None,\n        work_key: str | None = None, assignment_key: str | None = None,\n        a2a_task_id: str | None = None, harness_session_id: str | None = None,\n        actor_source: str | None = None, metadata: dict | None = None,\n        correlation_id: str | None = None,\n    ) -> AgentEventRecord:\n        seq = await _next_event_sequence(db)\n        rec = AgentEventRecord(\n            event_id=str(__import__(\"uuid\").uuid4()),\n            sequence=seq,\n            timestamp=_now(),\n            actor_source=actor_source,\n            agent_id=agent_id,\n            work_key=work_key,\n            assignment_key=assignment_key,\n            a2a_task_id=a2a_task_id,\n            harness_session_id=harness_session_id,\n            summary=summary[:512],\n            metadata_json=json.dumps(metadata) if metadata else None,\n            correlation_id=correlation_id,\n        )\n        db.add(rec)\n\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 0.25}

### [2026-09-29T10:13:34.950139+00:00] tool · tool: execute_code

{"status": "success", "output": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"migrate.py — ledger-based forward-only DB migration runner for LLM Manager.\n\nLedger table: schema_migrations(version TEXT PK, checksum TEXT, applied_at TIMESTAMPTZ, git_sha TEXT).\n\nUsage:\n  migrate.py status                     # show applied + pending\n  migrate.py apply  [--git-sha SHA]     # apply pending migrations in order\n  migrate.py verify                     # verify checksums of applied migrations\n\nRules (plan §8):\n- forward-only; destructive migrations are NEVER auto-applied (file must be\n  named NNN_destructive_*.sql and the runner refuses them outside --i-know-this-is-destructive)\n- never uploads/backups anywhere: backups are an operator action pre-migration\n\"\"\"\nimport hashlib\nimport os\nimport sys\n\nimport psycopg2\n\nMIGRATIONS_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\n\n# Connection inputs come from env only — never from code or files committed here.\n# LLM_MANAGER_PG_DSN      full DSN, OR the four vars below\n# LLM_MANAGER_PG_HOST / _DB / _USER / _PASSWORD\n\n\ndef connect():\n    dsn = os.environ.get(\"LLM_MANAGER_PG_DSN\")\n    if dsn:\n        return psycopg2.connect(dsn, connect_timeout=5)\n    host = os.environ.get(\"LLM_MANAGER_PG_HOST\", \"127.0.0.1\")\n    db = os.environ.get(\"LLM_MANAGER_PG_DB\", \"llmmanager\")\n    user = os.environ.get(\"LLM_MANAGER_PG_USER\", \"llmmanager\")\n    pw = os.environ.get(\"LLM_MANAGER_PG_PASSWORD\") or None\n    if pw is None:\n        # Same credential contract as the app (main.py _load_pg_pw): the\n        # PG_PW= line inside /etc/llm-manager/secrets/pg_app_creds. Keeping\n        # one source of truth means the transaction needs no duplicated\n        # secret env plumbing (plan §8).\n        pw_file = os.environ.get(\n            \"LLM_MANAGER_PG_PW_FILE\", \"/etc/llm-manager/secrets/pg_app_creds\")\n        try:\n            with open(pw_file) as f:\n                for line in f:\n                    if line.startswith(\"PG_PW=\"):\n                        pw = line.strip().split(\"=\", 1)[1]\n                        break\n        except OSError:\n            pw = None\n    return psycopg2.connect(host=host, dbname=db, user=user, password=pw or \"\", connect_timeout=5)\n\n\ndef ensure_ledger(cur):\n    cur.execute(\"\"\"\n        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_migrations (\n            version TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n            checksum TEXT NOT NULL,\n            applied_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),\n\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 0.02}

---- 1. resolve the work item (+ open assignment) ----------------------\n    work = await db.get(WorkItemRecord, work_item_id)\n    if work is None:\n        raise DispatchError(\"unknown-work-item\", 404)\n    assignments = await work_service.list_assignments(db, work_item_id=work_item_id)\n    active = next((a for a in assignments if a.status == \"ACTIVE\"), None)\n    if agent_id is None and active is None:\n        raise DispatchError(\"no-active-assignment\", 409)\n    target_agent = agent_id or active.agent_id\n    if active is not None and agent_id is not None and agent_id != active.agent_id:\n        raise DispatchError(\"agent-mismatch\", 409,\n                            {\"active_assignment_agent\": active.agent_id})\n\n    # ---- 2. idempotency: a retry must never double-dispatch ----------------\n    dedupe_key = _idempotency_key(work_item_id, active.assignment_id if active else None,\n                                  idempotency_key)\n    existing = await _find_existing_task(db, dedupe_key)\n    if existing is not None and existing.status == \"RUNNING\":\n        return {\"dispatched\": False, \"reason\": \"duplicate\",\n                \"task_id\": existing.task_id, \"external_task_id\": None,\n                \"budget_check\": None}\n\n    # ---- 3. budget gate (REQ-059) BEFORE any cloud execution ---------------\n    verdict = await budget_service.check_new_execution_allowed(db, work_item_id)\n    if not verdict.get(\"allowed\", True):\n        # hard exceeded without override → durable rejection event (Attention\n        # picks it up from the log; no separate mutable state) + hold dispatch.\n        await add_event(\n            db,\n            event_type=\"EXECUTION_REJECTED\",\n            actor_source=\"dispatch\",\n            agent_id=target_agent,\n            work_key=work.work_key,\n            assignment_key=active.assignment_key if active else None,\n            summary=(\"NEW cloud execution blocked: hard budget exceeded on \"\n                     f\"{work.work_key or work_item_id[:8]} \"\n                     f\"(state={verdict.get('budget_state')}; \"\n                     f\"override={'active' if (verdict.get('override') or {}).get('active') else 'none'})\")[:512],\n            metadata={\"budget_check\": verdict, \"dispatch_attempt\": True,\n                      \"work_item_id\": work_item_id, \"dedupe_key\": dedupe_key},\n        )\n        await db.commit()\n        return {\"dispatched\": False, \"reason\": \"hard_budget_exceeded\",\n                \"task_id\": None, \"external_task_id\": None,\n                \"budget_check\": verdict}\n\n    # ---- 4. resolve the bridge target --------------------------------------\n    bridge = get_bridge_for_agent(target_agent)\n    if bridge is None:\n        raise DispatchError(\"no-bridge-target\", 503, {\"agent_id\": target_agent})\n\n    # ---- 5. dispatch through the bridge (new A2A run) ----------------------\n    try:\n        result = bridge.send_work(\n            work_key=work.work_key or work_item_id,\n            assignment_key=(active.assignment_key if active else \"\") or \"\",\n            instruction=instruction,\n            session_id=session_id,\n        )\n    except BridgeError as e:\n        # dispatch itself failed — record it; budget NOT consumed (ACMS did not\n        # create a session), retry after the error is resolved.\n        await add_event(\n            db,\n            event_type=\"EXECUTION_FAILED\",\n            actor_source=\"dispatch\",\n            agent_id=target_agent,\n            work_key=work.work_key,\n            assignment_key=active.assignment_key if active else None,\n            summary=f\"A2A dispatch failed: {str(e)[:180]}\",\n            metadata={\"dedupe_key\": dedupe_key, \"budget_check\": verdict,\n                      \"work_item_id\": work_item_id},\n        )\n        await db.commit()\n        raise DispatchError(\"bridge-error\", 502, {\"detail\": str(e)[:300]}) from None\n\n    run_id = (result or {}).get(\"run_id\") or (result or {}).get(\"id\")\n\n    # ---- 6. record the execution task + correlation ------------------------\n    task, err = await work_service.create_execution_task(db, ExecutionTaskCreate(\n        work_item_id=work_item_id,\n        agent_id=target_agent,\n        external_task_id=f\"disp-{dedupe_key}\",\n    ))\n    if task is None:\n        raise DispatchError(err or \"task-record-failed\", 500)\n\n    # ---- 7. durable audit trail --------------------------------------------\n    await add_event(\n        db,\n        event_type=\"EXECUTION_DISPATCHED\",\n        actor_source=\"dispatch\",\n        agent_id=target_agent,\n        work_key=work.work_key,\n        assignment_key=active.assignment_key if active else None,\n        a2a_task_id=str(run_id) if run_id else None,\n        summary=(f\"Dispatched [{work.work_key or work_item_id[:8]}] to {str(target_agent)[:8]}\"\n                 + (f\" (soft exceeded: {verdict.get('budget_state')})\"\n                    if verdict.get(\"budget_state\") == \"SOFT_EXCEEDED\" else \"\"))[:512],", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}
